# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

# Independent verification ??relation map, bfcb83d

Reviewed HEAD: `bfcb83d4320877ffad47083c102ba1c38f691bba`, on 2026-11-03.

**Approval is blocked.** With the supplied database, the page can draw a refused distinct product/tracer as its originator, and can describe a stored trial collaborator as the lead sponsor. These are independently reproducible without changing any application or database file. Retirement fixtures expose two additional inconsistencies. Passing tests do not cover these cases.

## Scope, method, and limitations

- Reviewed the commit's five changed files and the relevant linker/graph-building code. The user brief is the specification; its local copy is [brief.md](brief.md).
- Ran the requested command, `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`, with bytecode writing disabled: **317 passed, 1 warning, 8.97 s**. The warning is a `cp949` decoding failure in a subprocess reader thread during `test_correction_check_fails_when_a_correction_is_pending`; it is not a failing assertion. Full output: [pytest.txt](pytest.txt).
- Started the unchanged application with `python -u src/app.py 8876`; exercised `GET /` and `POST /api/explore` over HTTP. The served page is byte-for-byte equal to `web/try.html`. The audit server was stopped afterward, and port 8876 was verified closed. Results and exact inputs: [http_results.json](http_results.json), [http_audit.py](http_audit.py).
- Compared `Engine.explore(id, per_group=100000)` against independently assembled database adjacency for **all 6,469 live nodes**, covering **18 centre/relation/direction/neighbour-type combinations**. This found **zero raw retrieval/value mismatches** in the current snapshot. It verifies stored relationships and values, not the correctness of the Korean wording or the claimed evidence ranking. Evidence: [db_validate.py](db_validate.py), [db_validate.json](db_validate.json).
- Retirement and malformed-property mutations occurred only in isolated in-memory databases. The real database was not changed. Source review, HTTP requests and fixture work called no model.
- Executed the actual page script in a Node VM with minimal DOM stubs and controlled HTTP response completion. Normal map payloads were also compared with actual HTTP results. This reproduces rendering strings, allocation, event handlers and asynchronous state; **it is not a real-browser rendering or exploit test**. Both available browser/native automation paths failed: `The browser access host is unavailable` and native pipe `os error 2`. No screenshot, browser-executed XSS, or browser layout claim is made. Dark mode was out of scope.
- No tracked file was edited. All review-created files are under `audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. An unrelated untracked `audit_evidence_claims/resume1/` directory appeared during review and was not touched. Git and database integrity results are recorded in [integrity.json](integrity.json).

## Standards review

No tracked `AGENTS.md`, `CONTRIBUTING`, or coding-standards document was found. The user-supplied instructions to ground conclusions in evidence and avoid tracked-file edits were followed. No separate coding-standard violation is asserted. The KOA/KhOpenApi code-change comparison instruction does not apply: this audit changes no application code.

## Specification blockers

### B1 ??P1: refused products and tracers become originator maps

Exact live-server reproductions:

| POST `/api/explore` JSON | Actual HTTP 200 result | Independent existing resolver result |
|---|---|---|
| `{"text":"bevacizumab - awwb"}` | `BEVACIZUMAB`, `CHEMBL1201583`, 122 trials | `Engine.resolve`: `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`; full-string `is_distinct_product` is true |
| `{"text":"89Zr - trastuzumab"}` | `TRASTUZUMAB`, `CHEMBL1201585`, 13 trials | `BLOCKED_CLASS_TERM`; full-string `is_radiotracer` is true |
| `{"text":"[89Zr] trastuzumab"}` | Same trastuzumab originator | Same tracer refusal |

Controls `bevacizumab-awwb` and `[89Zr]trastuzumab` are refused. Thus merely separating punctuation/qualifiers changes a refused product into the originator's relationship map.

**Cause:** `src/query.py:127-132` checks refusal on individual tokens. `src/query.py:146-168` subsequently joins matching windows and accepts aliases without applying the refusal checks to those windows. `src/app.py:81-88` trusts the first accepted hit. This is an inherited linker weakness exposed by the new map, not a claim that the tokenization bug originated in this commit. The map's promised product separation is nevertheless false (`src/app.py:72-74`, `web/try.html:184`).

**Required outcome:** accepted names must satisfy the same product/tracer boundaries as the existing resolver, including spaced and Unicode punctuation variants, before choosing a centre. Do not return an originator map for these exact inputs.

Evidence: [names_product_probe.py](names_product_probe.py), [names_product_bypass.json](names_product_bypass.json). No external pharmacology assumption is needed: the repository's own refusal predicates and resolver identify the prohibited product/tracer.

### B2 ??P1: collaborator is described as the trial lead

POST `{"text":"NCT00200395"}`. The returned `SPONSORS|in|Company` group includes Genentech. The page labels that group **?쒖씠 ?꾩긽??二쇨? 湲곌???*.

The exact live database edge is:

```text
ORG:genentech|SPONSORS|NCT00200395|NCT00200395
src = ORG:genentech
dst = NCT00200395
props.role = collaborator
retired_at = NULL
```

Montefiore Medical Center is stored as this trial's `lead_sponsor`. The page erases this distinction. In the reverse view it also calls a collaborator's trial **?쒖씠怨녹씠 二쇨????꾩긽??*.

**Cause and evidence for the distinction:** `src/graph.py:255-256` processes both lead sponsors and collaborators; `src/graph.py:282-286` stores their role. `src/graph.py:336-344` deliberately restricts the derived `SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF` relation to `lead_sponsor`. But `src/app.py:105-120` omits `SPONSORS.role`, and `web/try.html:144,194` applies the same lead-sponsor wording to all such edges. The supplied graph contains **1,652 live collaborator SPONSORS edges**, alongside 3,000 lead-sponsor edges.

**Required outcome:** preserve and display the role, using accurate incoming/outgoing wording or a neutral combined heading. A collaborator must not be asserted to be the lead.

Evidence: [db_fixture.json](db_fixture.json), [ui_real_maps.json](ui_real_maps.json), [ui_harness_results.json](ui_harness_results.json).

### B3 ??P2: investigator is described as an institution

POST `{"text":"NCT00754364"}`. The group `SPONSORS|in|Investigator` contains `ORG:armandosantoromd`, **Armando Santoro Md**, but its heading is again **?쒖씠 ?꾩긽??二쇨? 湲곌???*. This is a person, not a stored Company/institution. There are 43 live Investigator ??SPONSORS ??Trial edges.

`src/graph.py:260-269` deliberately distinguishes people from institutions. `web/try.html:140` has the correct Investigator translation, but `web/try.html:144,194` selects the heading using only relation and direction. This is a concrete type/relationship wording failure within the requested Investigator-vs-Company check.

**Required outcome:** use person-appropriate or neutral wording for this group. Evidence: [db_fixture.json](db_fixture.json), [ui_harness_results.json](ui_harness_results.json).

### B4 ??P2, isolated retirement state: a retired centre returns through name lookup

The supplied snapshot has no retired Drugs. To test the requested retirement behavior, [names_retired_http.py](names_retired_http.py) backs up the real database into memory and applies only:

```sql
UPDATE node SET retired_at='AUDIT_FIXTURE' WHERE id='CHEMBL3353410';
```

Through the real HTTP handler:

- POST `{"text":"osimertinib"}` returns HTTP 200 with that **retired** centre and four groups with totals **79, 53, 1, 1**.
- POST `{"text":"CHEMBL3353410"}` returns `not_found`.
- An `EGFR` query excludes the retired drug as a neighbour.

`src/app.py:76-77` checks retirement only for the exact-ID path. Dictionary-based name resolution bypasses that check, and the final node query at `src/app.py:89` has no retirement predicate. Thus a retired node can still be explored by name, despite being excluded by ID and from its neighbours' maps.

**Required outcome:** apply a live-node check to the resolved centre regardless of resolution path. This is a reproduced lifecycle defect, **not current-snapshot retired-drug leakage**. Evidence: [names_retired_http_results.json](names_retired_http_results.json).

## Additional retirement-count defect

An isolated fixture has live Drug `D`, retired Trial `T`, live `T -EVALUATES-> D`, and live `D -ACTS_ON-> X`. Real-handler POST `{"text":"D"}` returns `center.trials=1` while excluding T from the groups; POST `{"text":"X"}` gives D a trial weight of 1.

`src/app.py:100-102` counts live EVALUATES edges without checking their source Trial, whereas `src/app.py:90-95` excludes retired neighbour nodes. If ?쒖씠 洹몃옒?????꾩긽??means the displayed live graph, this is an incorrect live-trial count and should be fixed by using live Trial endpoints. If the intended definition is explicitly *all non-retired EVALUATES rows even when their trials are retired*, the count matches that narrower SQL definition, but the page needs to state that distinction. This finding does not depend on any retired edge being counted: retired edges themselves are correctly excluded.

**Current-snapshot qualification:** there are zero live edges with retired endpoints, so this inconsistency is not present in the supplied database. Evidence: [db_fixture.json](db_fixture.json), [db_http_fixtures.json](db_http_fixtures.json). The verdict already fails on B1?밄3 independently of this counting-contract interpretation.

### URL safety qualification

`esc` protects label/title/id text and quoted attributes in the tested HTML/SVG payloads. Node IDs, not edge IDs, populate `data-id`; edge IDs are escaped text (`web/try.html:171,195-196`). No markup/attribute breakout was found for the tested `<script>`, `<img onerror>`, quotes, or SVG-title payloads.

However, `web/try.html:193,196` applies HTML escaping to URLs without a scheme allowlist. Supplying fixture `props.url = "javascript:alert(document.domain)"` leaves that exact scheme in both generated anchors. **This establishes unsafe URL acceptance, not confirmed JavaScript execution**: the links have `target="_blank" rel="noopener"`, and browser navigation behavior was not tested because the browser tools were unavailable. All **3,000 current stored node URLs** use `https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/`; no current malicious stored URL was found ([url_scan.json](url_scan.json)). Restrict allowed URL schemes and test in a real browser before claiming universal injection safety. Evidence: [ui_harness_results.json](ui_harness_results.json).

## Coverage and successful checks

### A / C ??graph membership, directions, values

- Current DB has **7 retired Company nodes and 142 retired edges**, including 18 retired EVALUATES edges. They did not leak into current live-neighbour results. No current self-loop or edge with both ends equal to a centre was found. The SQL's self-loop behavior is one outgoing item, not two copies (`src/app.py:93,120`); this was inspected, not a current-data reproduction.
- All six current node types are in TKO/TCOL. No current uncovered type was found. Unknown future types use raw/fallback labels in some places but yield `undefined` in the SVG tooltip; extensibility is not proved.
- PARENT_OF, STUDIES, ASSOCIATED_WITH, EVALUATES and ACTS_ON directions match their stored endpoints. Examples retained in `db_validate.json` include parent and child Company views, Trial?묭isease, Disease?뭈rial, Target?묭isease and Disease?뭈arget. The SPONSORS wording failures are B2/B3; its raw direction is correct.
- Returned trial phases/status/start/title/url, non-sponsor Drug max phase/count, sponsor `evidence_trials`/class, and ACTS_ON action agree with stored data. Group totals and descending group sizes are correct. Raw ordering agrees with the implemented weights, with the wording qualification above.
- A **1,001-Drug** fixture spans three count chunks. `explore('X', per_group=2000)` returns all 1,001 items with weight exactly 1; no chunk boundary failure was found (`src/app.py:98-102`, [db_fixture.json](db_fixture.json)).

### B ??names and boundaries

- `Mvasi` and `Mvasi bevacizumab` refuse; `no such thing zz` is not found. `Mvasi and bevacizumab` selects the explicitly named bevacizumab but omits the refused span from the response. This is incomplete disclosure, distinct from B1's product-to-originator bypass.
- Salt and parent records remain distinct: Tarceva / erlotinib hydrochloride ??`CHEMBL1079742` (31 trials); erlotinib ??`CHEMBL553` (118).
- `  OsImErTiNiB\n` works over HTTP; fullwidth `竊∽섬竊∽설竊댐섄竊? resolves. Lowercase NCT IDs are not recognized. Investigator IDs work, but investigator names are absent from the linker. These are usability limitations, not invented relationships.
- Current company index has **zero** multi-ID alias entries, so the loss of `also_matching_ids` has no supplied-snapshot reproduction. The code does discard it. No claim is made that this ambiguity cannot arise after a rebuild.
- HTTP input is stripped and silently truncated to 500 characters (`src/app.py:182`). Exact input `"x" * 500 + " osimertinib"` returns not_found for the 500-character prefix; `"osimertinib " + "x" * 600` draws osimertinib. This should be disclosed or rejected explicitly.

### D / F ??size, errors, concurrency

- Disease `MONDO_0005233`: **2,774 Trials + 300 Targets**, 30 returned per list, **17,158 UTF-8 response bytes**, **77.319 ms** in the recorded ordinary HTTP call. The ring contains 39 neighbours; list totals remain true. This is one local timing, not a load benchmark.
- Empty object/text, whitespace and null text return **400** with `empty input`; malformed JSON returns 400. A 21,000-character text body returns 400 because only the first 20,000 bytes are parsed. JSON list/null bodies return 400 with raw `.get` errors. Valid text at an unknown route returns 404. Subsequent requests remain usable.
- **24 simultaneous requests with 12 workers**, mixing drugs, targets, a company, a refused name, Disease and Trial, all returned the expected 200 response shapes in **633.145 ms** total. `src/app.py:188-204` serializes engine work with the lock. This shows the exercised workload succeeds; it does not imply concurrent UI responses are ordered.

### E / G ??read-only behavior and documentation

- The explore path issues SELECTs, and `link_entities` reads cached dictionaries/SELECTs; no model call occurs. Existing question answering explicitly sets `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:136-137`). Database SHA-256 before and after the HTTP run was identical: `7341c5a7089731f67356da5a588b269d4b69e8e6c4c054acf19aa4ef2837b529`. The connection is opened read/write (`src/app.py:39`), so read-only behavior is observed/code-path based, not enforced by SQLite mode.
- README command descriptions at `README.md:566` and `README.ko.md:387` accurately advertise a relation-map page; no additional blanket accuracy claim was found in those lines.
- Module `src/app.py:5` and `explore`'s `src/app.py:70-74` overstate ?쐃verything??and evidence ordering when paired with capped output. `web/try.html:54` overstates the ring's completeness. Explain the two cuts and per-relation sorting. `src/app.py:10` also says ?쏛ll three??after introducing four boxes, a minor pre-existing documentation inconsistency.
- PH renders stored trial phase codes without finding a false phase translation in current data; unmapped codes such as `NA` stay raw. ACT and sponsor-class qualifications are listed above.

## New specification blockers

### R1 ??P1, current database: refusal windows select the refused product and change QA's subject

Exact live-server request:

```json
{"text":"osimertinib and generic version of bevacizumab"}
```

`POST /api/explore` returns HTTP 200 with **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583, 122 trials**, and `also=[{"refused":"osimertinib and generic version of"}]`. With the exact old linker restored, the selected centre is OSIMERTINIB. Independently, the unchanged `/api/resolve` returns `osimertinib` as EXACT / CHEMBL3353410 and `generic version of bevacizumab` as **BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT**. The map is therefore drawing the component the same application explicitly refuses. This conclusion uses repository rules, not an external pharmacology assumption.

**Causal change:** the new hunk at `src/query.py:153-162` applies a refusal predicate to longest-first windows (`146-150`) and marks the whole window refused. The first five tokens contain `generic version`, which satisfies `src/textrules.py:149`, but omit bevacizumab. Their refusal consumes osimertinib. The overlapping window containing the actual qualified product is skipped at `src/query.py:148-149`, leaving bevacizumab available for alias acceptance at `171-178`. `src/app.py:87-95` then selects that remaining hit.

The same regression reaches QA:

| Exact `/api/ask` text | Old-linker first evidence | Current first evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `Which trials evaluate osimertinib and generic version of bevacizumab?` | `NCT03909334\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL3353410\|NCT03909334` | `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
| `Which companies sponsor osimertinib and generic version of bevacizumab?` | `ORG:astrazeneca\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL3353410\|` | `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |

Both old/current trial answers have 12 evidence rows; success codes and evidence counts alone miss the subject switch. Related over-refusal occurs for `[89Zr] trastuzumab and osimertinib`: the new linker refuses the whole input and QA produces no evidence, whereas `osimertinib and [89Zr] trastuzumab` retains osimertinib and its evidence. `osimertinib and oral azacitidine` similarly loses the separately named valid drug.

Controls: `generic version of bevacizumab` alone now correctly refuses; `osimertinib and bevacizumab` selects osimertinib and discloses bevacizumab; reversing the mixed generic-product input retains osimertinib. `What is the generic version of bevacizumab?` also maps bevacizumab, but did so before: that specific bypass is inherited, not attributed to this repair.

**Required outcome:** bind refusal to the actual product/tracer span and preserve separately named valid entities regardless of order. The mixed generic-product input must never select BEVACIZUMAB. Evidence: [isolated before/after HTTP comparisons](names_regressions.json), [boundary controls](names_boundary_details.json), [independent actual-server confirmation](regression_http.json). These cases are absent from the four added tests at `tests/test_app.py:57-92`; passing the existing suite/demos does not resolve them.

### R2 ??P2, isolated malformed-data state: an unknown sponsor count becomes a displayed zero

Back up the real DB into memory and change only the props of this live edge:

```text
ORG:alaunos|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF|CHEMBL1201438|
```

Use `UPDATE edge SET props=? WHERE id=?` with each literal JSON/text value `null`, `[]`, `{broken`. Request `{"text":"CHEMBL1201438"}` and `{"text":"ORG:alaunos"}` through the real handler.

At bfcb83d these requests fail closed with 400. At HEAD each returns 200; that edge has `weight:0`, and the actual current script renders **`梨낆엫 二쇨????꾩긽 0嫄?**. After the fixture mutation there is **no stored numeric evidence_trials value**. The original unmutated value was 1, but it is not legitimate to treat that removed value as still stored, nor to replace the unavailable value with a factual zero.

**Cause:** new `_obj` (`src/app.py:198-204`) converts invalid/non-object props to `{}` at line 129; the existing `ep.get("evidence_trials") or 0` at line 136 now turns newly accepted invalid data into a zero; `web/try.html:173` presents the sorting weight as the stored count. This is an interaction introduced by the parser repair. Empty `{}` already defaulted to zero before; the newly demonstrated null/list/broken-JSON paths previously returned errors.

**Required outcome:** keep an unavailable count distinct from zero. A separate default sort weight is fine, but the factual detail must say unknown/not recorded or return a controlled data error. No malformed props exist in the supplied snapshot; this is a fixture-only failure within the brief's explicit malformed-JSON scope. Evidence: [baseline/current real-handler fixture](db_missing_sponsor_count.json), [reproducer](db_missing_sponsor_count.py), [actual page rendering](ui_missing_sponsor_count.json).

## Additional should-fix items and qualifications

1. **New boolean-score rendering failure, fixture only.** For a live Target?묨SSOCIATED_WITH?묭isease edge with `{"association_score":true}` (or false), `src/app.py:145` accepts Python bool as numeric. HTTP returns a boolean score; new `web/try.html:176` calls `.toFixed(3)` and throws. Rendering occurs outside the fetch catch (`213`), leaving `洹몃━??以묅? and a pushed trail entry. All 300 current association scores are JSON real. Validate numeric type excluding bool and handle rendering errors. [API baseline/current fixture](db_score_fixture.json), [script reproduction](ui_regressions.json).
2. **New score precision is undisclosed.** `EGFR` has stored/API score `0.8883707400322897`, displayed `0.888`; `KRAS` has `0.8337881598550857`, displayed `0.834` (`web/try.html:176`). This is rounding, not corrupt stored data or a fabricated relationship. Mark approximation/precision and expose the exact value if the stored-value promise includes this new field. It is listed separately from R2's invented zero. [Exact/rendered pairs](ui_regressions.json).
3. **Company ambiguity repair is incomplete for embedded aliases.** Current company index has zero multi-ID aliases. An index-only fixture sets `companies['sharedbrand']=['ORG:astrazeneca','ORG:roche']`: `Sharedbrand` offers Roche, but `Sharedbrand company` returns `also=[]` despite the linker's `also_matching_ids=['ORG:roche']`. `src/app.py:95-107` checks the whole input instead of consuming the chosen hit's alternatives (`src/query.py:189-197`). No current-snapshot multi-ID case is claimed. [Fixture](names_boundary_details.json).
4. **Residual documentation/input limits** are the NOT FIXED rows above: module ?쐃verything??and successful-input truncation. The brief's README command descriptions remain accurate at `README.md:566` and `README.ko.md:387`.

## Specification blockers

### M3-B1 ??P1: the new joiner exclusion lets a refused product description draw its originator

Exact unchanged-server requests:

```text
POST /api/explore
{"text":"paclitaxel with albumin bound nanoparticles"}
```

HTTP 200 returns:

```json
{"center":{"id":"CHEMBL428647","label":"PACLITAXEL","trials":234},
 "also":[{"refused":"albumin bound nanoparticles"}]}
```

This is an excerpt; [supplemental_http.json](supplemental_http.json) preserves the complete response. `/api/resolve` for the **same complete input** returns `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no drug ID. The unmodified local predicate `is_distinct_product` also returns true. Restoring only the immediately prior `c64f433` linker makes the map refuse the whole input, with no centre. Thus this is a repair regression on the supplied database, not a fixture or an external pharmacology inference. `paclitaxel with albumin bound formulation` and `generic version with bevacizumab` are additional same-cause reproductions.

The regression also changes QA: `What trials evaluate paclitaxel with albumin bound nanoparticles?` now returns **12 PACLITAXEL trial evidence rows**, first `NCT00019019|EVALUATES|CHEMBL428647|NCT00019019`; the immediately prior linker returns **0**. [Isolated before/after comparison](names_new_boundaries.json), [independent actual-server evidence](supplemental_http.json).

**Causal hunk:** `src/query.py:92-93` includes `with` in `_JOINERS`; `src/query.py:170-172` suppresses the multi-token refusal whenever any token is a joiner. The previous condition checked `is_distinct_product(window)` without that exemption. For this five-token input, the entire product is no longer refused; the later three-token suffix is refused, leaving `paclitaxel` available to the alias acceptance at `src/query.py:181-195`. `src/app.py:88-95` selects that hit. The predicate's `albumin[\s\-]?bound` rule is at `src/textrules.py:145`, applied at `207-208`.

The control `Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid` still correctly selects the stored PACLITAXEL POLIGLUMEX alias (`CHEMBL2108628`) in both revisions. All 21 joiner-containing stored alias records retain identical answers. This does **not** establish that every `with` is an entity separator; the failed example is a refused full product description, not a broken ordinary alias. [Alias scan and control](names_new_boundaries.json).

**Required outcome:** distinguish separators between named entities from words inside a single qualified product. Refusal must cover the product's parent drug, while preserving separately named accepted entities. The exact product description above must not select CHEMBL428647 or answer with its trial evidence. Keep the repaired mixed-osimertinib cases and the valid poliglumex alias working.

### M3-B2 ??P1, inherited: a leading question phrase still detaches the generic qualifier from its drug

`POST /api/explore {"text":"What is the generic version of bevacizumab?"}` returns **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583, 122 trials**, with `also=[{"refused":"What is the generic version"}]`. The same full input at `/api/resolve` is `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`. This exact bypass was explicitly documented in m2b and remains reproduced; it is **not introduced by HEAD**. [HTTP evidence](supplemental_http.json), [previous/current linker comparison](names_new_boundaries.json).

The five-token longest-first refusal at `src/query.py:157-178` consumes the question prefix because it contains `generic version`, before reaching the qualified drug. The overlapping actual product span is then skipped, and the remaining bevacizumab token links. Qualifier recognition alone is insufficient: the refused span must include the substance it qualifies. This is another known path violating the same approval bar, independently of the new `with` regression.

## Additional should-fix and unchanged limitations

1. **Comma boundary repair is incomplete in the single-token refusal pass.** `osimertinib, liposomal doxorubicin` returns `refused:'osimertinib liposomal doxorubicin'`; reversing the order retains OSIMERTINIB. The same occurs for `osimertinib, biosimilar bevacizumab`. The unchanged resolver separates osimertinib as EXACT and refuses only the second product. QA `Which trials evaluate osimertinib, biosimilar bevacizumab?` returns no evidence. `src/query.py:139-143` unconditionally adds adjacent token indexes without checking the new `clause` boundary; the new guard applies only later at `170-172`. This is inherited over-refusal, not a new originator bypass. `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md:27` therefore overstates that a refused span never crosses a comma. [Before/after](names_new_boundaries.json), [HTTP](supplemental_http.json).
2. Lowercase IDs / Investigator-name lookup and raw malformed-request error details remain the unchanged qualifications marked NOT FIXED above. Empty/whitespace map form submissions still make no request and leave the previous view; this is unchanged, not a repair regression. [UI checks](ui_m3_checks.json).
3. `_number` excludes bool and NaN but still accepts positive/negative infinity; `allow_nan=False` then returns controlled HTTP 400. No invalid numeric value is drawn, and none occurs in the supplied snapshot. This is a failure-closed limitation, not a blocker. [Numeric controls](db_numeric_fixture.json).

 succeeded in 2300ms:
---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# Independent verification — relation map, bfcb83d

Reviewed HEAD: `bfcb83d4320877ffad47083c102ba1c38f691bba`, on 2026-11-03.

**Approval is blocked.** With the supplied database, the page can draw a refused distinct product/tracer as its originator, and can describe a stored trial collaborator as the lead sponsor. These are independently reproducible without changing any application or database file. Retirement fixtures expose two additional inconsistencies. Passing tests do not cover these cases.

## Scope, method, and limitations

- Reviewed the commit's five changed files and the relevant linker/graph-building code. The user brief is the specification; its local copy is [brief.md](brief.md).
- Ran the requested command, `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`, with bytecode writing disabled: **317 passed, 1 warning, 8.97 s**. The warning is a `cp949` decoding failure in a subprocess reader thread during `test_correction_check_fails_when_a_correction_is_pending`; it is not a failing assertion. Full output: [pytest.txt](pytest.txt).
- Started the unchanged application with `python -u src/app.py 8876`; exercised `GET /` and `POST /api/explore` over HTTP. The served page is byte-for-byte equal to `web/try.html`. The audit server was stopped afterward, and port 8876 was verified closed. Results and exact inputs: [http_results.json](http_results.json), [http_audit.py](http_audit.py).
- Compared `Engine.explore(id, per_group=100000)` against independently assembled database adjacency for **all 6,469 live nodes**, covering **18 centre/relation/direction/neighbour-type combinations**. This found **zero raw retrieval/value mismatches** in the current snapshot. It verifies stored relationships and values, not the correctness of the Korean wording or the claimed evidence ranking. Evidence: [db_validate.py](db_validate.py), [db_validate.json](db_validate.json).
- Retirement and malformed-property mutations occurred only in isolated in-memory databases. The real database was not changed. Source review, HTTP requests and fixture work called no model.
- Executed the actual page script in a Node VM with minimal DOM stubs and controlled HTTP response completion. Normal map payloads were also compared with actual HTTP results. This reproduces rendering strings, allocation, event handlers and asynchronous state; **it is not a real-browser rendering or exploit test**. Both available browser/native automation paths failed: `The browser access host is unavailable` and native pipe `os error 2`. No screenshot, browser-executed XSS, or browser layout claim is made. Dark mode was out of scope.
- No tracked file was edited. All review-created files are under `audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. An unrelated untracked `audit_evidence_claims/resume1/` directory appeared during review and was not touched. Git and database integrity results are recorded in [integrity.json](integrity.json).

## Standards review

No tracked `AGENTS.md`, `CONTRIBUTING`, or coding-standards document was found. The user-supplied instructions to ground conclusions in evidence and avoid tracked-file edits were followed. No separate coding-standard violation is asserted. The KOA/KhOpenApi code-change comparison instruction does not apply: this audit changes no application code.

## Specification blockers

### B1 — P1: refused products and tracers become originator maps

Exact live-server reproductions:

| POST `/api/explore` JSON | Actual HTTP 200 result | Independent existing resolver result |
|---|---|---|
| `{"text":"bevacizumab - awwb"}` | `BEVACIZUMAB`, `CHEMBL1201583`, 122 trials | `Engine.resolve`: `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`; full-string `is_distinct_product` is true |
| `{"text":"89Zr - trastuzumab"}` | `TRASTUZUMAB`, `CHEMBL1201585`, 13 trials | `BLOCKED_CLASS_TERM`; full-string `is_radiotracer` is true |
| `{"text":"[89Zr] trastuzumab"}` | Same trastuzumab originator | Same tracer refusal |

Controls `bevacizumab-awwb` and `[89Zr]trastuzumab` are refused. Thus merely separating punctuation/qualifiers changes a refused product into the originator's relationship map.

**Cause:** `src/query.py:127-132` checks refusal on individual tokens. `src/query.py:146-168` subsequently joins matching windows and accepts aliases without applying the refusal checks to those windows. `src/app.py:81-88` trusts the first accepted hit. This is an inherited linker weakness exposed by the new map, not a claim that the tokenization bug originated in this commit. The map's promised product separation is nevertheless false (`src/app.py:72-74`, `web/try.html:184`).

**Required outcome:** accepted names must satisfy the same product/tracer boundaries as the existing resolver, including spaced and Unicode punctuation variants, before choosing a centre. Do not return an originator map for these exact inputs.

Evidence: [names_product_probe.py](names_product_probe.py), [names_product_bypass.json](names_product_bypass.json). No external pharmacology assumption is needed: the repository's own refusal predicates and resolver identify the prohibited product/tracer.

### B2 — P1: collaborator is described as the trial lead

POST `{"text":"NCT00200395"}`. The returned `SPONSORS|in|Company` group includes Genentech. The page labels that group **“이 임상의 주관 기관”**.

The exact live database edge is:

```text
ORG:genentech|SPONSORS|NCT00200395|NCT00200395
src = ORG:genentech
dst = NCT00200395
props.role = collaborator
retired_at = NULL
```

Montefiore Medical Center is stored as this trial's `lead_sponsor`. The page erases this distinction. In the reverse view it also calls a collaborator's trial **“이곳이 주관한 임상”**.

**Cause and evidence for the distinction:** `src/graph.py:255-256` processes both lead sponsors and collaborators; `src/graph.py:282-286` stores their role. `src/graph.py:336-344` deliberately restricts the derived `SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF` relation to `lead_sponsor`. But `src/app.py:105-120` omits `SPONSORS.role`, and `web/try.html:144,194` applies the same lead-sponsor wording to all such edges. The supplied graph contains **1,652 live collaborator SPONSORS edges**, alongside 3,000 lead-sponsor edges.

**Required outcome:** preserve and display the role, using accurate incoming/outgoing wording or a neutral combined heading. A collaborator must not be asserted to be the lead.

Evidence: [db_fixture.json](db_fixture.json), [ui_real_maps.json](ui_real_maps.json), [ui_harness_results.json](ui_harness_results.json).

### B3 — P2: investigator is described as an institution

POST `{"text":"NCT00754364"}`. The group `SPONSORS|in|Investigator` contains `ORG:armandosantoromd`, **Armando Santoro Md**, but its heading is again **“이 임상의 주관 기관”**. This is a person, not a stored Company/institution. There are 43 live Investigator → SPONSORS → Trial edges.

`src/graph.py:260-269` deliberately distinguishes people from institutions. `web/try.html:140` has the correct Investigator translation, but `web/try.html:144,194` selects the heading using only relation and direction. This is a concrete type/relationship wording failure within the requested Investigator-vs-Company check.

**Required outcome:** use person-appropriate or neutral wording for this group. Evidence: [db_fixture.json](db_fixture.json), [ui_harness_results.json](ui_harness_results.json).

### B4 — P2, isolated retirement state: a retired centre returns through name lookup

The supplied snapshot has no retired Drugs. To test the requested retirement behavior, [names_retired_http.py](names_retired_http.py) backs up the real database into memory and applies only:

```sql
UPDATE node SET retired_at='AUDIT_FIXTURE' WHERE id='CHEMBL3353410';
```

Through the real HTTP handler:

- POST `{"text":"osimertinib"}` returns HTTP 200 with that **retired** centre and four groups with totals **79, 53, 1, 1**.
- POST `{"text":"CHEMBL3353410"}` returns `not_found`.
- An `EGFR` query excludes the retired drug as a neighbour.

`src/app.py:76-77` checks retirement only for the exact-ID path. Dictionary-based name resolution bypasses that check, and the final node query at `src/app.py:89` has no retirement predicate. Thus a retired node can still be explored by name, despite being excluded by ID and from its neighbours' maps.

**Required outcome:** apply a live-node check to the resolved centre regardless of resolution path. This is a reproduced lifecycle defect, **not current-snapshot retired-drug leakage**. Evidence: [names_retired_http_results.json](names_retired_http_results.json).

## Additional retirement-count defect

An isolated fixture has live Drug `D`, retired Trial `T`, live `T -EVALUATES-> D`, and live `D -ACTS_ON-> X`. Real-handler POST `{"text":"D"}` returns `center.trials=1` while excluding T from the groups; POST `{"text":"X"}` gives D a trial weight of 1.

`src/app.py:100-102` counts live EVALUATES edges without checking their source Trial, whereas `src/app.py:90-95` excludes retired neighbour nodes. If “이 그래프 안 임상” means the displayed live graph, this is an incorrect live-trial count and should be fixed by using live Trial endpoints. If the intended definition is explicitly *all non-retired EVALUATES rows even when their trials are retired*, the count matches that narrower SQL definition, but the page needs to state that distinction. This finding does not depend on any retired edge being counted: retired edges themselves are correctly excluded.

**Current-snapshot qualification:** there are zero live edges with retired endpoints, so this inconsistency is not present in the supplied database. Evidence: [db_fixture.json](db_fixture.json), [db_http_fixtures.json](db_http_fixtures.json). The verdict already fails on B1–B3 independently of this counting-contract interpretation.

## Should-fix findings

| Finding and exact reproduction | Evidence / code |
|---|---|
| **“Most evidence” is not the implemented ordering.** `MONDO_0005233` selects trials by descending start-date string, led by `NCT05745740` / `2027-12-01`. Its 300 associated Targets have weight 0 and are selected by reverse label order (`ZFHX3`, `XPC`, …), ignoring stored association scores. Sponsor-derived groups do use `evidence_trials`; ordinary Drug groups use EVALUATES counts. Describe each actual sort instead of implying every retained item has the most evidence. | `src/app.py:74,108-124`; `web/try.html:194`; [db_validate.json](db_validate.json) |
| **The ring silently cuts further than the lists.** `CHEMBL1201438` / ALDESLEUKIN returns 25 items (11 trials, 11 sponsors, 3 targets), all lists below 30, but `drawMap` draws only 22 neighbours. No list truncation notice appears, while the introduction says “모두 그립니다”. Show ring drawn/total separately. No whole nonempty group is lost: the allocation takes at least 3 available items from each. `MAX=40` is not a hard cap; AstraZeneca draws 41. | `web/try.html:54,160-165,194`; [ui_harness_results.json](ui_harness_results.json) |
| **Stale trail buttons fail after refusal/not-found/error.** Navigate osimertinib → EGFR, then submit Mvasi, `unknown-m1-entity`, or a request returning an error in the VM harness. Submission clears the trail array, but the early return leaves old buttons. Clicking old back index 0 throws `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading '0')`. | `web/try.html:183-188,199-202`; [ui_harness.js](ui_harness.js), [ui_harness_results.json](ui_harness_results.json) |
| **Overlapping responses can replace the latest selection with an older one.** Start EGFR then KRAS; complete KRAS before EGFR. The final map becomes EGFR and the trail follows completion order. Two overlapping EGFR calls append duplicate entries. This is a deterministic controlled-response reproduction, not a claim that network reordering was observed in the browser. | `web/try.html:181-197,200`; [ui_harness_results.json](ui_harness_results.json) |
| **Identity ambiguity is silent.** `Roche` draws FOLIC ACID (`CHEMBL1622`), whereas `ORG:roche` draws the company. `MET` selects METFORMIN. `osimertinib EGFR` selects osimertinib; reversing the order selects EGFR. These reflect stored aliases/linker priority, so they are not fabricated edges, but show the selected match and alternatives or require an explicit choice. | `src/query.py:155-187`; `src/app.py:82-88`; [names_results.json](names_results.json), [names_label_scan.json](names_label_scan.json) |
| **Action/class rendering loses specificity.** ALDESLEUKIN has three `AGONIST` target edges, rendered as “작용: 작용”. Preserve `AGONIST` or a specific Korean label. `BINDING_AGENT` does not match stored `BINDING AGENT`; raw-code fallback is accurate but untranslated. Sponsor classes are collapsed into INDUSTRY/non-industry wording; actual codes FED, NETWORK, NIH, OTHER, OTHER_GOV are not visible. No current UNKNOWN-class counterexample was found. | `web/try.html:149-155`; [ui_harness_results.json](ui_harness_results.json), [db_probe.json](db_probe.json) |
| **Some stored details are omitted.** A centre Trial's `start_date` is returned but not displayed. A Drug under a sponsor's `SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF` group shows sponsor-specific trial count/class, omitting that Drug's global count/max phase because the sponsor branch wins. These are omissions, not altered stored values. Zero-trial Drug centres return `trials:null`, omitting the count rather than showing 0. | `src/app.py:107-115,130-133`; `web/try.html:151-155,191-192` |
| **Malformed props fail closed, but with raw implementation errors.** In isolated real-handler fixtures, node/edge props `null`, `[]`, and `{broken` return 400 with AttributeError/JSONDecodeError text. Each restored request returns 200, so the lock recovers. Validate object shape or provide a clearer data-error response. None of these invalid props occur in the supplied snapshot. | `src/app.py:105,128,208-210`; [db_http_fixtures.json](db_http_fixtures.json) |

### URL safety qualification

`esc` protects label/title/id text and quoted attributes in the tested HTML/SVG payloads. Node IDs, not edge IDs, populate `data-id`; edge IDs are escaped text (`web/try.html:171,195-196`). No markup/attribute breakout was found for the tested `<script>`, `<img onerror>`, quotes, or SVG-title payloads.

However, `web/try.html:193,196` applies HTML escaping to URLs without a scheme allowlist. Supplying fixture `props.url = "javascript:alert(document.domain)"` leaves that exact scheme in both generated anchors. **This establishes unsafe URL acceptance, not confirmed JavaScript execution**: the links have `target="_blank" rel="noopener"`, and browser navigation behavior was not tested because the browser tools were unavailable. All **3,000 current stored node URLs** use `https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/`; no current malicious stored URL was found ([url_scan.json](url_scan.json)). Restrict allowed URL schemes and test in a real browser before claiming universal injection safety. Evidence: [ui_harness_results.json](ui_harness_results.json).

## Coverage and successful checks

### A / C — graph membership, directions, values

- Current DB has **7 retired Company nodes and 142 retired edges**, including 18 retired EVALUATES edges. They did not leak into current live-neighbour results. No current self-loop or edge with both ends equal to a centre was found. The SQL's self-loop behavior is one outgoing item, not two copies (`src/app.py:93,120`); this was inspected, not a current-data reproduction.
- All six current node types are in TKO/TCOL. No current uncovered type was found. Unknown future types use raw/fallback labels in some places but yield `undefined` in the SVG tooltip; extensibility is not proved.
- PARENT_OF, STUDIES, ASSOCIATED_WITH, EVALUATES and ACTS_ON directions match their stored endpoints. Examples retained in `db_validate.json` include parent and child Company views, Trial→Disease, Disease→Trial, Target→Disease and Disease→Target. The SPONSORS wording failures are B2/B3; its raw direction is correct.
- Returned trial phases/status/start/title/url, non-sponsor Drug max phase/count, sponsor `evidence_trials`/class, and ACTS_ON action agree with stored data. Group totals and descending group sizes are correct. Raw ordering agrees with the implemented weights, with the wording qualification above.
- A **1,001-Drug** fixture spans three count chunks. `explore('X', per_group=2000)` returns all 1,001 items with weight exactly 1; no chunk boundary failure was found (`src/app.py:98-102`, [db_fixture.json](db_fixture.json)).

### B — names and boundaries

- `Mvasi` and `Mvasi bevacizumab` refuse; `no such thing zz` is not found. `Mvasi and bevacizumab` selects the explicitly named bevacizumab but omits the refused span from the response. This is incomplete disclosure, distinct from B1's product-to-originator bypass.
- Salt and parent records remain distinct: Tarceva / erlotinib hydrochloride → `CHEMBL1079742` (31 trials); erlotinib → `CHEMBL553` (118).
- `  OsImErTiNiB\n` works over HTTP; fullwidth `ＡＶＡＳＴＩＮ` resolves. Lowercase NCT IDs are not recognized. Investigator IDs work, but investigator names are absent from the linker. These are usability limitations, not invented relationships.
- Current company index has **zero** multi-ID alias entries, so the loss of `also_matching_ids` has no supplied-snapshot reproduction. The code does discard it. No claim is made that this ambiguity cannot arise after a rebuild.
- HTTP input is stripped and silently truncated to 500 characters (`src/app.py:182`). Exact input `"x" * 500 + " osimertinib"` returns not_found for the 500-character prefix; `"osimertinib " + "x" * 600` draws osimertinib. This should be disclosed or rejected explicitly.

### D / F — size, errors, concurrency

- Disease `MONDO_0005233`: **2,774 Trials + 300 Targets**, 30 returned per list, **17,158 UTF-8 response bytes**, **77.319 ms** in the recorded ordinary HTTP call. The ring contains 39 neighbours; list totals remain true. This is one local timing, not a load benchmark.
- Empty object/text, whitespace and null text return **400** with `empty input`; malformed JSON returns 400. A 21,000-character text body returns 400 because only the first 20,000 bytes are parsed. JSON list/null bodies return 400 with raw `.get` errors. Valid text at an unknown route returns 404. Subsequent requests remain usable.
- **24 simultaneous requests with 12 workers**, mixing drugs, targets, a company, a refused name, Disease and Trial, all returned the expected 200 response shapes in **633.145 ms** total. `src/app.py:188-204` serializes engine work with the lock. This shows the exercised workload succeeds; it does not imply concurrent UI responses are ordered.

### E / G — read-only behavior and documentation

- The explore path issues SELECTs, and `link_entities` reads cached dictionaries/SELECTs; no model call occurs. Existing question answering explicitly sets `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:136-137`). Database SHA-256 before and after the HTTP run was identical: `7341c5a7089731f67356da5a588b269d4b69e8e6c4c054acf19aa4ef2837b529`. The connection is opened read/write (`src/app.py:39`), so read-only behavior is observed/code-path based, not enforced by SQLite mode.
- README command descriptions at `README.md:566` and `README.ko.md:387` accurately advertise a relation-map page; no additional blanket accuracy claim was found in those lines.
- Module `src/app.py:5` and `explore`'s `src/app.py:70-74` overstate “everything” and evidence ordering when paired with capped output. `web/try.html:54` overstates the ring's completeness. Explain the two cuts and per-relation sorting. `src/app.py:10` also says “All three” after introducing four boxes, a minor pre-existing documentation inconsistency.
- PH renders stored trial phase codes without finding a false phase translation in current data; unmapped codes such as `NA` stay raw. ACT and sponsor-class qualifications are listed above.

# Independent relation-map review — round 2 re-run

Reviewed HEAD: `c64f433648ad252b24254030196048fc17e81006`, 2026-11-03. Repair commits: `9db058c` and `c64f433`; comparison: `git diff bfcb83d...HEAD`. Specification: the user's round-2 brief and [m1 report](../m1/REPORT.md). The interrupted m2 scripts were inspected, relocated and rerun; their old outputs are not treated as current verification.

**Approval remains blocked.** B1–B4's exact original reproductions and the retired-trial count are fixed. However, the new refusal-window logic can discard a valid drug and draw the distinct product that the application's resolver refuses. It also changes question-answering evidence to that wrong subject. Separately, the malformed-props repair can display an unavailable sponsor count as a factual zero in an isolated fixture.

## New specification blockers

### R1 — P1, current database: refusal windows select the refused product and change QA's subject

Exact live-server request:

```json
{"text":"osimertinib and generic version of bevacizumab"}
```

`POST /api/explore` returns HTTP 200 with **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583, 122 trials**, and `also=[{"refused":"osimertinib and generic version of"}]`. With the exact old linker restored, the selected centre is OSIMERTINIB. Independently, the unchanged `/api/resolve` returns `osimertinib` as EXACT / CHEMBL3353410 and `generic version of bevacizumab` as **BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT**. The map is therefore drawing the component the same application explicitly refuses. This conclusion uses repository rules, not an external pharmacology assumption.

**Causal change:** the new hunk at `src/query.py:153-162` applies a refusal predicate to longest-first windows (`146-150`) and marks the whole window refused. The first five tokens contain `generic version`, which satisfies `src/textrules.py:149`, but omit bevacizumab. Their refusal consumes osimertinib. The overlapping window containing the actual qualified product is skipped at `src/query.py:148-149`, leaving bevacizumab available for alias acceptance at `171-178`. `src/app.py:87-95` then selects that remaining hit.

The same regression reaches QA:

| Exact `/api/ask` text | Old-linker first evidence | Current first evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `Which trials evaluate osimertinib and generic version of bevacizumab?` | `NCT03909334\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL3353410\|NCT03909334` | `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
| `Which companies sponsor osimertinib and generic version of bevacizumab?` | `ORG:astrazeneca\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL3353410\|` | `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |

Both old/current trial answers have 12 evidence rows; success codes and evidence counts alone miss the subject switch. Related over-refusal occurs for `[89Zr] trastuzumab and osimertinib`: the new linker refuses the whole input and QA produces no evidence, whereas `osimertinib and [89Zr] trastuzumab` retains osimertinib and its evidence. `osimertinib and oral azacitidine` similarly loses the separately named valid drug.

Controls: `generic version of bevacizumab` alone now correctly refuses; `osimertinib and bevacizumab` selects osimertinib and discloses bevacizumab; reversing the mixed generic-product input retains osimertinib. `What is the generic version of bevacizumab?` also maps bevacizumab, but did so before: that specific bypass is inherited, not attributed to this repair.

**Required outcome:** bind refusal to the actual product/tracer span and preserve separately named valid entities regardless of order. The mixed generic-product input must never select BEVACIZUMAB. Evidence: [isolated before/after HTTP comparisons](names_regressions.json), [boundary controls](names_boundary_details.json), [independent actual-server confirmation](regression_http.json). These cases are absent from the four added tests at `tests/test_app.py:57-92`; passing the existing suite/demos does not resolve them.

### R2 — P2, isolated malformed-data state: an unknown sponsor count becomes a displayed zero

Back up the real DB into memory and change only the props of this live edge:

```text
ORG:alaunos|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF|CHEMBL1201438|
```

Use `UPDATE edge SET props=? WHERE id=?` with each literal JSON/text value `null`, `[]`, `{broken`. Request `{"text":"CHEMBL1201438"}` and `{"text":"ORG:alaunos"}` through the real handler.

At bfcb83d these requests fail closed with 400. At HEAD each returns 200; that edge has `weight:0`, and the actual current script renders **`책임 주관한 임상 0건`**. After the fixture mutation there is **no stored numeric evidence_trials value**. The original unmutated value was 1, but it is not legitimate to treat that removed value as still stored, nor to replace the unavailable value with a factual zero.

**Cause:** new `_obj` (`src/app.py:198-204`) converts invalid/non-object props to `{}` at line 129; the existing `ep.get("evidence_trials") or 0` at line 136 now turns newly accepted invalid data into a zero; `web/try.html:173` presents the sorting weight as the stored count. This is an interaction introduced by the parser repair. Empty `{}` already defaulted to zero before; the newly demonstrated null/list/broken-JSON paths previously returned errors.

**Required outcome:** keep an unavailable count distinct from zero. A separate default sort weight is fine, but the factual detail must say unknown/not recorded or return a controlled data error. No malformed props exist in the supplied snapshot; this is a fixture-only failure within the brief's explicit malformed-JSON scope. Evidence: [baseline/current real-handler fixture](db_missing_sponsor_count.json), [reproducer](db_missing_sponsor_count.py), [actual page rendering](ui_missing_sponsor_count.json).

## Additional should-fix items and qualifications

1. **New boolean-score rendering failure, fixture only.** For a live Target→ASSOCIATED_WITH→Disease edge with `{"association_score":true}` (or false), `src/app.py:145` accepts Python bool as numeric. HTTP returns a boolean score; new `web/try.html:176` calls `.toFixed(3)` and throws. Rendering occurs outside the fetch catch (`213`), leaving `그리는 중…` and a pushed trail entry. All 300 current association scores are JSON real. Validate numeric type excluding bool and handle rendering errors. [API baseline/current fixture](db_score_fixture.json), [script reproduction](ui_regressions.json).
2. **New score precision is undisclosed.** `EGFR` has stored/API score `0.8883707400322897`, displayed `0.888`; `KRAS` has `0.8337881598550857`, displayed `0.834` (`web/try.html:176`). This is rounding, not corrupt stored data or a fabricated relationship. Mark approximation/precision and expose the exact value if the stored-value promise includes this new field. It is listed separately from R2's invented zero. [Exact/rendered pairs](ui_regressions.json).
3. **Company ambiguity repair is incomplete for embedded aliases.** Current company index has zero multi-ID aliases. An index-only fixture sets `companies['sharedbrand']=['ORG:astrazeneca','ORG:roche']`: `Sharedbrand` offers Roche, but `Sharedbrand company` returns `also=[]` despite the linker's `also_matching_ids=['ORG:roche']`. `src/app.py:95-107` checks the whole input instead of consuming the chosen hit's alternatives (`src/query.py:189-197`). No current-snapshot multi-ID case is claimed. [Fixture](names_boundary_details.json).
4. **Residual documentation/input limits** are the NOT FIXED rows above: module “everything” and successful-input truncation. The brief's README command descriptions remain accurate at `README.md:566` and `README.ko.md:387`.

Other Unicode/product boundary variants accepted by both the unchanged resolver and linker are not misrepresented as repair regressions. - Final HEAD, tracked/staged diff checks, database/source hashes, report links and audit-port closure are recorded in [integrity.json](integrity.json). No application remediation was applied.

## Reproduction entry points

All commands below write only within m2b; use the repository root as the working directory. The HTTP runner owns and closes port 8878; `regression_http.py` and `ui_http_check.py` expect the unchanged app already running there. Other fixture scripts own their ephemeral HTTP servers.

```text
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m2b/run_verification.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m2b/run_http.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m2b/db_recheck.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m2b/db_role_fallback.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m2b/db_missing_sponsor_count.py

python -B audit_evidence_claims/m2b/names_reproduction.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m2b/names_regressions.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m2b/names_boundary_details.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m2b/ui_collect.py
node audit_evidence_claims/m2b/ui_harness.js
node audit_evidence_claims/m2b/ui_regressions.js
node audit_evidence_claims/m2b/ui_missing_sponsor_count.js
```

Supporting independent reviews: [DB](db_findings.md), [names/QA](names_findings.md), [page/standards](ui_findings.md). Standards axis: 0 documented violations. Specification axis: 2 blockers (R1 current-data product/QA subject switch; R2 fixture-only false sponsor count), with should-fix items separated above.

17: ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
18: PORT = 8879
19: BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
20: sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
21: os.environ["PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE"] = "1"
22: DB = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
23: 
24: def git(*args):
25:     p = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
26:     return {"code": p.returncode, "stdout": p.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
27:             "stderr": p.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}
28: 
29: def post(path, payload):
30:     req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
31:                                  headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
32:     try:
33:         res = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
34:     except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
35:         res = e
36:     return {"status": res.status, "body": json.loads(res.read())}
37:

40:            "db_sha256": before}

42: with socket.socket() as s:
43:     assert s.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "audit port already occupied"
44: 
45: log = (OUT / "server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8")
46: proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)], cwd=ROOT,
47:                         env=os.environ.copy(), stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
48:                         creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
49: try:
50:     for _ in range(100):
51:         if proc.poll() is not None:
52:             raise RuntimeError("server exited")
53:         try:
54:             urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
55:             break
56:         except OSError:
57:             time.sleep(.1)
58:     else:
59:         raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")

61:     original = (ROOT / "audit_evidence_claims/m1/http_audit.py").read_text(encoding="utf-8")

64:     run = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", str(OUT / "http_audit.py")], cwd=ROOT,
65:                          capture_output=True, env={**os.environ, "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8"})
