import query
import textrules as T
from app import Engine

OUT = Path('audit_evidence_claims/m3')
cur, old = Engine(), Engine()
source = subprocess.check_output(['git', 'show', 'c64f433:src/query.py']).decode('utf-8')
method = next(n for n in ast.walk(ast.parse(source)) if isinstance(n, ast.FunctionDef) and n.name == 'link_entities')
namespace = dict(query.__dict__)
exec(compile(textwrap.dedent(ast.get_source_segment(source, method)), 'c64f433:src/query.py', 'exec'), namespace)
old.kg.link_entities = types.MethodType(namespace['link_entities'], old.kg)

inputs = [
    'paclitaxel with albumin bound nanoparticles',
    'paclitaxel with nanoparticle albumin bound formulation',
    'paclitaxel with albumin bound formulation',
    'What trials evaluate paclitaxel with albumin bound nanoparticles?',
    'paclitaxel with albumin-bound nanoparticles',
    'generic version with bevacizumab',
    'generic version with paclitaxel',
    'generic version with osimertinib',
    'bevacizumab with albumin-bound nanoparticles',
    'Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid',
    'generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid',
    'daratumumab and hyaluronidase-fihj',
    'osimertinib, liposomal doxorubicin',
    'Mvasi, osimertinib',
    'osimertinib, Mvasi',
    'liposomal doxorubicin, osimertinib',
    'osimertinib, biosimilar bevacizumab',
    'biosimilar bevacizumab, osimertinib',
    'osimertinib and liposomal doxorubicin',
    'osimertinib, generic version of bevacizumab',
    'generic version of bevacizumab, osimertinib',
    'osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab',
    'generic version of bevacizumab with osimertinib',
    'bevacizumab, - awwb',
    '[89Zr], trastuzumab',
    'Paclitaxel conjugate, with poly-l-glutamic acid',
    'What is the generic version of bevacizumab?',
    'osimertinib; generic version of bevacizumab',
    'osimertinib， generic version of bevacizumab',
    'Which trials evaluate osimertinib, biosimilar bevacizumab?',
]

def result(engine, value):
    mapped = engine.explore(value)
    return {
        'linker': engine.kg.link_entities(value),
        'map': {k: v for k, v in mapped.items() if k != 'groups'},
        'answer': engine.ask(value),
    }

rows = []
for value in inputs:
    rows.append({
        'input': value,
        'distinct_product': T.is_distinct_product(value),
        'radiotracer': T.is_radiotracer(value),
        'resolver': cur.resolve(value),
        'current': result(cur, value),
        'previous_linker_c64f433': result(old, value),
    })

aliases = []
for key, hits in cur.dic['index'].items():
    for hit in hits:
        if re.search(r'\b(with|and|or|plus|vs|then|without|followed)\b', hit['alias'], re.I):
            aliases.append({
                'key': key, 'stored_hit': hit,
                'current': result(cur, hit['alias']),
                'previous_linker_c64f433': result(old, hit['alias']),
            })

data = {'baseline': 'c64f433648ad252b24254030196048fc17e81006 link_entities only',
        'cases': rows, 'joiner_aliases': aliases}
OUT.joinpath('names_new_boundaries.json').write_text(json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
for row in rows:
    print(json.dumps({
        'input': row['input'],
        'current': row['current']['map'],
        'previous': row['previous_linker_c64f433']['map'],
        'resolver': [(c['component'], c['tier'], c.get('drug_id')) for c in row['resolver']['components']],
        'current_evidence': len(row['current']['answer']['evidence']),
        'previous_evidence': len(row['previous_linker_c64f433']['answer']['evidence']),
    }, ensure_ascii=False))
print(json.dumps({'joiner_alias_count': len(aliases), 'joiner_alias_answer_differences': [r['stored_hit']['alias'] for r in aliases if r['current']['answer'] != r['previous_linker_c64f433']['answer']]}))
cur.kg.conn.close()
old.kg.conn.close()

+## Specification blockers
+
+### M4-B1 — P1, new regression: equal-length binding refuses the wrong drug
+
+Exact unchanged-server request:
+
+```text
+POST /api/explore
+{"text":"osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab"}
+```
+
+HTTP 200 contains:
+
+```json
+{"center":{"id":"CHEMBL1201583","label":"BEVACIZUMAB","trials":122},
+ "also":[{"refused":"osimertinib with generic version"}]}
+```
+
+This is an excerpt of the full response in [attack_http.json](attack_http.json). With the exact previous linker and previous `_JOINERS`, the centre is **OSIMERTINIB / CHEMBL3353410** and the refused span is **generic version of bevacizumab**. The current full-string resolver returns `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no drug ID. Reversing the drugs (`bevacizumab with generic version of osimertinib`) also selects the qualified second drug. Adding just `a` — `osimertinib with a generic version of bevacizumab` — changes the current result back to OSIMERTINIB. This is a distance/tie artifact, not a product-identity distinction.
+
+| Exact `/api/ask` text | Previous linker's first evidence | Current HTTP first evidence |
+|---|---|---|
+| `Which trials evaluate osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` | `NCT03909334\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL3353410\|NCT03909334` | `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
+| `Which companies sponsor osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` | `ORG:astrazeneca\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL3353410\|` | `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |
+
+Both current answers have 12 evidence rows; status/evidence-count checks alone miss the subject switch. [Current HTTP](attack_http.json), [before/after name and QA evidence](names_findings.md).
+
+**Cause:** `with` was removed from `_JOINERS` (`src/query.py:91-95`). `_drug_spans` supplies the first drug before the second (`222-239`). Both `osimertinib with generic version` and `generic version of bevacizumab` are four-token candidate spans. The shortest-span selector iterates drug spans in that order and updates only on strictly shorter length (`255-272`), so the first candidate wins. Its qualifier tokens enter `out`; the correct second candidate is then disallowed by `span & out` (`264`). BEVACIZUMAB remains acceptable at `181-189`, and the map takes it at `src/app.py:88-95`.
+
+**Required outcome:** bind the qualifier to the product it describes without consuming a separately named valid drug. This input must never map or answer for generic bevacizumab as BEVACIZUMAB. Preserve the repaired albumin-bound paclitaxel case; globally reinstating `with` as a separator would reopen m3-B1.
+
+### M4-B2 — P1: six-token cap admits qualified longer aliases and distant qualifiers
+
+`POST /api/explore {"text":"generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid"}` now draws **PACLITAXEL POLIGLUMEX / CHEMBL2108628**, with `also:[]`. The same full input is `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` at `/api/resolve`; previous `574097f` refuses with no centre. This exact string was already a m3 supplementary control. [Full current HTTP](attack_http.json), [exact previous/current comparison](names_attack.json).
+
+**Cause:** the stored alias occupies five tokens. `_drug_spans` deliberately retains that full non-overlapping name (`src/query.py:222-239`), but `_qualified_drug_spans` only examines spans up to **six tokens** (`258`: `range(b - a, 7)`). The qualifier plus `of` plus the full alias requires eight. No tested span can contain both `generic version` and the complete drug, so nothing is refused. The map then accepts the bare full alias inside the refused product description. This is a new regression even though the bare poliglumex alias correctly remains supported.
+
+The cap also leaves a simpler inherited bypass: `generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab` (seven tokens) draws BEVACIZUMAB with `also:[]`; the six-token control `generic version of the antibody bevacizumab` refuses. QA `Which trials evaluate generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab?` returns 12 BEVACIZUMAB trial rows. `What targets does generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid act on?` also returns current evidence for CHEMBL2108628 (12 rows, first ACTS_ON edge), despite the qualified product being unresolved. These are exact HTTP observations; the report does not assume every QA-prefix variant was safe before. [Responses](attack_http.json).
+
+**Required outcome:** an arbitrary token-distance ceiling must not turn a known qualified product into a positive originator match. Preserve valid bare multi-token aliases and fail safely when qualification cannot be bound reliably.
+
+### M4-B3 — P1, inherited: the single-token prepass blocks its own extension to the drug
+
+| Exact `/api/explore` text | Current result | Current `/api/ask` reproduction |
+|---|---|---|
+| `biosimilar version of bevacizumab` | BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583; only `biosimilar version` refused | `Which trials evaluate biosimilar version of bevacizumab?` → 12 BEVACIZUMAB trial rows |
+| `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin` | DOXORUBICIN / CHEMBL53463; only `liposomal formulation` refused | `Which trials evaluate liposomal formulation of doxorubicin?` → 3 DOXORUBICIN trial rows |
+
+The existing resolver refuses both complete products. Both parent leaks also occur with `574097f`, so these are **inherited**, not attributed to the latest repair. They nevertheless defeat the approval bar and the new claim that refusal is bound to its drug. [Current HTTP](attack_http.json), [before/after](names_attack.json).
+
+**Cause:** `src/query.py:143-151` marks the qualifier and immediate neighbour before binding. `_drug_spans(tokens, refused)` skips those tokens (`252`, `225-229`), and every candidate crossing them is rejected by `span & refused` (`264`). Thus the helper cannot extend a previously recognized qualifier across `version of` or `formulation of` to its drug. These examples fit within six tokens; raising only the cap does not fix them.
+
+The two-product input `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin and biosimilar version of bevacizumab` links **both parent drugs**, selects DOXORUBICIN and puts BEVACIZUMAB in `also`. By contrast, adjacent `liposomal doxorubicin and biosimilar bevacizumab` correctly refuses both. [Two-product controls](names_attack.json).
+
+**Required outcome:** single-token and multi-token qualification need one consistent binding process; already refused qualifier tokens must not make their own qualified drug available as a positive hit.
+
+## No-relink and question-answering assessment
+
+The refusal blockers above reproduce even when matched spans may be relinked in an otherwise identical HEAD linker. They are not caused by the no-relink repair.
+
+- All **7 actual demo questions**, sent over HTTP, have complete response objects identical to the original `bfcb83d` query module. They are also identical to immediate predecessor `574097f` in the focused QA comparison. [HTTP demo evidence](demo_results.json), [focused comparison](names_attack.json).
+- Across **2,584 unique multi-token alias keys**, 402 entity lists change; none changes the first accepted identity or introduces a new Drug. The common change removes an embedded parent Drug/company name. Bare salt/full-name identity is preserved. [Alias comparison](names_attack.json).
+- The isolated counterfactual uses HEAD's exact helpers, dictionaries and data, and changes only `(refused | matched)` back to `refused` in the overlap guard (`src/query.py:168`). **1,648 questions**: 28 completely identical, 1,596 entity-metadata-only changes, 24 semantic changes, 0 exceptions. It covers all 402 changed aliases under four QA templates, plus 40 varied questions and eight extra company/target controls. [Reproducer](qa_relink_sweep.py), [all inputs and changed answers](qa_relink_sweep.json).
+- The 24 semantic changes remove a Company read from inside a stored full Drug alias, switching `company_portfolio` to `drug_profile`. For example `Tell me about GSK 1120212` previously used nested GSK and returned 12 GlaxoSmithKline portfolio rows; current returns TRAMETINIB's three profile/mechanism rows. `Tell me about EPOETIN BETA` stops treating BETA as company `ORG:beta`. These changes agree with the stored full aliases; they are not adverse regressions found by this review.
+- Explicit `What trials evaluate erlotinib hydrochloride and erlotinib?` still links both separately named records. Multi-entity target/company questions and mixed accepted/refused inputs were checked separately; passing demo/salt checks do not excuse the subject-switch failures above.
+
+**Conclusion for this isolated change:** no adverse no-relink QA regression was found in the exercised corpus. This is a bounded result, not proof for every possible sentence.
+

+1. **Inherited over-refusal of a neighbouring target/company.** `EGFR biosimilar bevacizumab` and `AstraZeneca biosimilar bevacizumab` refuse the entire input; the reversed forms retain EGFR/AstraZeneca. The generic-version equivalents now retain the target/company. This follows the unconditional immediate-neighbour rule at `src/query.py:149-151`; it does not identify the neighbour's type or syntactic role. ## N1 — P1 new regression: shortest span can bind the qualifier to the other drug

Exact input: `osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab`.

HEAD returns centre BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583, 122 trials, and `also=[{"refused":"osimertinib with generic version"}]`. The immediately previous linker returns OSIMERTINIB / CHEMBL3353410 and refuses `generic version of bevacizumab`. Reversing the drug names gives the symmetric failure: `bevacizumab with generic version of osimertinib` selects OSIMERTINIB and refuses the bevacizumab prefix. The existing resolver refuses the complete product-containing input; there is no external pharmacological assumption.

`_drug_spans` discovers each drug separately. `_qualified_drug_spans` iterates the left drug first (`src/query.py:255`). Both `osimertinib with generic version` and `generic version of bevacizumab` have length four and satisfy the product predicate. The strict `<` update (`271-272`) retains the left candidate. `out` then prevents the right drug from using the qualifier (`264`), so ordinary linking accepts it (`181-189`). Removing `with` from `_JOINERS` (`92-96`) enabled this regression. A shortest token span does not establish semantic attachment.

QA on the plain input now gives BEVACIZUMAB profile/VEGFA evidence instead of OSIMERTINIB profile/EGFR evidence. `names_final_checks.json` verifies the exact full questions against 574097f: `Which trials evaluate osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` changes its first edge from `NCT03909334|EVALUATES|CHEMBL3353410|NCT03909334` to `NCT00955305|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1201583|NCT00955305`; `Which companies sponsor osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` changes AstraZeneca/osimertinib to Roche/bevacizumab. Both retain 12 rows. Root's `attack_http.json` also exercises these over the unchanged CLI server. This fails the refusal/subject guarantee regardless of whether a graph edge is itself real.

Evidence: `names_attack.json` (full current/previous/counterfactual results), `names_new_boundaries.json` (rerun prior supplementary case).

## N2 — P1 new regression plus inherited remote-qualifier failures: six-token ceiling

Exact new regression: `generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid`.

The five-token suffix is a stored unambiguous alias for PACLITAXEL POLIGLUMEX / CHEMBL2108628. Unqualified, it correctly selects that entity in both versions. Qualified, HEAD still selects it, shows 4 graph trials, and reports no refused span. `Engine.ask` returns 7 profile/mechanism evidence rows; the 574097f linker refuses and returns zero. The unchanged resolver reports `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` for the full input.

Before/after qualification: the plain phrase above is the new map/QA regression. The longer question `What targets does generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid act on?` already bypassed the previous linker and returns the same 12 target evidence rows in both versions. `Which trials evaluate generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid?` likewise returns 4 in both. Those longer-question failures are inherited, not newly attributed. Exact comparisons are in `names_final_checks.json`.

The helper requires a candidate to include the entire matched drug span, while `range(b-a, 7)` (`src/query.py:258`) permits only up to six tokens. The full eight-token phrase cannot be examined; `generic version` is not a single-token predicate, so nothing in the prefix is refused either. Ordinary longest-first alias matching accepts all five drug tokens (`166-189`). The no-relink counterfactual still selects the same centre: this failure is the refusal algorithm, not removing nested aliases.

Inherited remote-qualifier cases expose the same ceiling: `generic version of the anticancer drug bevacizumab` (seven tokens) and `generic version of the commercially available monoclonal antibody bevacizumab` select BEVACIZUMAB. The six-token control `generic version of the drug bevacizumab` refuses. `paclitaxel formulated with nanoparticles that are albumin bound` selects PACLITAXEL. These full strings satisfy the existing product predicate; the long generic bevacizumab case also produces 12 bevacizumab trial evidence rows when asked as a trial question. Such remote failures existed before, but HEAD now omits even the old detached qualifier refusal.

Evidence: `names_attack.json`, `names_attack.py`; server confirmation in root's `attack_http.json`.

## N3 — P1 inherited failure: pre-refused qualifier cannot extend to its drug

`biosimilar version of bevacizumab` selects BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583 and refuses only `biosimilar version`. `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin` selects DOXORUBICIN / CHEMBL53463 and refuses only `liposomal formulation`. Both complete inputs are `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` at the unchanged resolver. Trial questions return 12 bevacizumab rows and 3 doxorubicin rows respectively. Before and after results are identical in this essential failure, so it is not attributed as a new regression.

The token pass at `src/query.py:143-151` initially refuses each qualifier and its immediate neighbours. The helper rejects every candidate touching that `refused` set (`264`); it therefore cannot connect the known qualifier to the later named drug, even within four tokens. `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin and biosimilar version of bevacizumab` links both originators. In contrast, `liposomal doxorubicin and biosimilar bevacizumab` correctly refuses both products.

Hyphenated `paclitaxel with albumin-bound nanoparticles` is an already exercised same-mechanism control from m3: the one-token albumin-bound pass refuses its neighbours but leaves PACLITAXEL. The repaired unhyphenated `albumin bound` case does not establish the broader guarantee.

## Other design attacks and unchanged limitations

- Two qualified drugs: `generic version of bevacizumab and generic version of osimertinib` and the `with` variant refuse both. Mixed attached `liposomal doxorubicin and biosimilar bevacizumab` also refuses both. Remote single-token qualifier variants fail as N3.
- Qualifier between two drugs: N1 gives a clear `with ... of` attachment counterexample. `bevacizumab generic version osimertinib` picks the left span and retains osimertinib; this text is semantically ambiguous, so it is a mechanism probe rather than an additional independent blocker.
- Targets/companies near qualifiers: `EGFR generic version of bevacizumab` and `AstraZeneca generic version of bevacizumab` now preserve the target/company. `EGFR biosimilar bevacizumab` and `AstraZeneca biosimilar bevacizumab` still swallow those entities; reverse order preserves them. This is inherited over-refusal and a should-fix, distinct from originator leakage. `src/query.py:149-151` still grabs any adjacent non-joiner, including non-Drug entities.
- `Roche generic version of bevacizumab` selects bevacizumab after binding the qualifier to Roche's stored FOLIC ACID drug alias. This is a related alias-collision amplification of N1, not a claim that the dictionary's Roche alias is new.
- Semicolons/fullwidth commas are not list boundaries: `osimertinib; generic version of bevacizumab` and `osimertinib， generic version of bevacizumab` still select bevacizumab. Normal comma fixes do not extend to these separators.
- M1 Unicode/unknown controls and long HTTP inputs were rerun. Product spellings accepted by the resolver itself (e.g. `ABP 215`, `89Zr trastuzumab`, unsupported Unicode suffix punctuation) are recorded, not misrepresented as a new linker/resolver mismatch.

## Matched-span non-relinking isolation

This change is tested independently from the refusal changes. A separate Engine uses exactly HEAD with only `(refused | matched)` replaced by `refused` in `link_entities`; `_qualified_drug_spans`, `_drug_spans`, globals, retrieval, data and model setting remain identical.

- `names_attack.json`: 45 focused attack inputs; 37 varied QA questions including all 7 distinct page/CLI demos; **all 7 demo responses identical to 574097f**. Of the 37 ordinary QA controls, 13 differ only in entity metadata, and none has a different evidence list. Controls cover drug salts, parent+salt explicitly repeated, companies, targets, disease names, multi-entity order and multi-token aliases.
- The same artifact scans **2,584 multi-token unambiguous alias keys**. **402** change their linked entity lists, but no first accepted identity changes and no new Drug appears. Salt parents, compound-word companies and embedded targets are no longer read from an already accepted alias. This is scoped to those stored keys, not every possible sentence.
- `qa_relink_sweep.json`: four question templates for all 400 alias keys changed specifically by the no-relink toggle, plus 48 company/target/mixed controls: **1,648 questions; 28 identical responses, 1,596 entity-metadata-only changes, 24 semantic changes, zero exceptions**.
- Every semantic change is `company_portfolio` → `drug_profile` after removing a nested Company hit from an accepted Drug alias. Example: `Tell me about GSK 1120212` previously supplies 12 GlaxoSmithKline portfolio rows; HEAD supplies 3 TRAMETINIB profile/mechanism rows. `Tell me about EPOETIN BETA` previously sees Company Beta and returns no evidence; HEAD returns the named drug's two profile/mechanism rows. These observed changes support the intended repair. No adverse QA regression attributable specifically to non-relinking was found in this sweep.

`qa_relink_sweep.py` retains every generated question and full results for every semantic difference. The sweep intentionally records entity-only changes separately so a changed entity count is not mistaken for changed evidence. New refusal failures N1/N2 persist in the no-relink counterfactual and therefore cannot be assigned to this separate repair.

## Required result

Retain the exact fixed earlier cases and intended removal of nested aliases, while ensuring a qualifier belongs to the right named drug and can still reach that drug across ordinary descriptive words. The current implementation has two new prior-control regressions and inherited product bypasses. Names/specification axis recommends **CHANGES REQUIRED**.

"""Isolate no-relink on all aliases whose link result differs plus company/mixed questions."""
import ast,json,sqlite3,sys,textwrap,types
from pathlib import Path
sys.path.insert(0,str(Path('src').resolve()))
import query
from app import Engine
OUT=Path('audit_evidence_claims/m4')
cur=Engine(); old=Engine()
src=Path('src/query.py').read_text(encoding='utf8')
method=next(n for n in ast.walk(ast.parse(src)) if isinstance(n,ast.FunctionDef) and n.name=='link_entities')
ns=dict(query.__dict__)
exec(compile(textwrap.dedent(ast.get_source_segment(src,method)).replace('(refused | matched)','refused'),'HEAD-with-relink','exec'),ns)
old.kg.link_entities=types.MethodType(ns['link_entities'],old.kg)
for e in (cur,old):
    e.kg.conn.close();e.kg.conn=sqlite3.connect('file:data/processed/oncokg.sqlite?mode=ro',uri=True);e.kg.conn.row_factory=sqlite3.Row
aliases=json.loads(OUT.joinpath('names_attack.json').read_text(encoding='utf8'))['multi_token_aliases']
changed=[r['input'] for r in aliases if r['current']!=r['relink']]
inputs=[template.format(name) for name in changed for template in ['Tell me about {}','Which trials evaluate {}?','Who sponsors {}?','What targets does {} act on?']]
inputs += [template.format(name) for name in ['AstraZeneca','National Cancer Institute','Bristol Myers Squibb','Johnson & Johnson','Pfizer','GlaxoSmithKline','MD Anderson Cancer Center','GSK 1120212 and EGFR','osimertinib and erlotinib hydrochloride','erlotinib hydrochloride and osimertinib','AstraZeneca and erlotinib hydrochloride','erlotinib hydrochloride and AstraZeneca'] for template in ['Tell me about {}','Which trials evaluate {}?','Who sponsors {}?','What targets does {} act on?']]
rows=[]
counts={'questions':len(inputs),'identical':0,'entities_only':0,'semantic_changes':0,'errors':0}
for s in inputs:
    try:
        a,b=cur.ask(s),old.ask(s)
        # Effects on answer/evidence/intent matter separately from pruned entity metadata.
        sema={k:v for k,v in a.items() if k!='entities'}
        semb={k:v for k,v in b.items() if k!='entities'}
        kind='identical' if a==b else 'entities_only' if sema==semb else 'semantic_changes'
        counts[kind]+=1
        if kind=='semantic_changes':rows.append({'input':s,'current':a,'relink':b})
    except Exception as ex:
        counts['errors']+=1;rows.append({'input':s,'error':repr(ex)})
out={'baseline':'HEAD identical except matched tokens may be linked again','summary':counts,'inputs':inputs,'changed_answers':rows}
OUT.joinpath('qa_relink_sweep.json').write_text(json.dumps(out,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps(counts))
for r in rows:
    if 'error' in r:print(r);continue
    print(r['input'],r['current']['intent'],'<-',r['relink']['intent'],len(r['current']['evidence']),len(r['relink']['evidence']))
for e in (cur,old):e.kg.conn.close()

+## Specification blockers
+
+### M4-B1 — P1, new regression: equal-length binding refuses the wrong drug
+
+Exact unchanged-server request:
+
+```text
+POST /api/explore
+{"text":"osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab"}
+```
+
+HTTP 200 contains:
+
+```json
+{"center":{"id":"CHEMBL1201583","label":"BEVACIZUMAB","trials":122},
+ "also":[{"refused":"osimertinib with generic version"}]}
+```
+
+This is an excerpt of the full response in [attack_http.json](attack_http.json). With the exact previous linker and previous `_JOINERS`, the centre is **OSIMERTINIB / CHEMBL3353410** and the refused span is **generic version of bevacizumab**. The current full-string resolver returns `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no drug ID. Reversing the drugs (`bevacizumab with generic version of osimertinib`) also selects the qualified second drug. Adding just `a` — `osimertinib with a generic version of bevacizumab` — changes the current result back to OSIMERTINIB. This is a distance/tie artifact, not a product-identity distinction.
+
+| Exact `/api/ask` text | Previous linker's first evidence | Current HTTP first evidence |
+|---|---|---|
+| `Which trials evaluate osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` | `NCT03909334\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL3353410\|NCT03909334` | `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
+| `Which companies sponsor osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` | `ORG:astrazeneca\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL3353410\|` | `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |
+
+Both current answers have 12 evidence rows; status/evidence-count checks alone miss the subject switch. [Current HTTP](attack_http.json), [exact before/after QA](names_final_checks.json).
+
+**Cause:** `with` was removed from `_JOINERS` (`src/query.py:91-95`). `_drug_spans` supplies the first drug before the second (`222-239`). Both `osimertinib with generic version` and `generic version of bevacizumab` are four-token candidate spans. The shortest-span selector iterates drug spans in that order and updates only on strictly shorter length (`255-272`), so the first candidate wins. Its qualifier tokens enter `out`; the correct second candidate is then disallowed by `span & out` (`264`). BEVACIZUMAB remains acceptable at `181-189`, and the map takes it at `src/app.py:88-95`.
+
+**Required outcome:** bind the qualifier to the product it describes without consuming a separately named valid drug. This input must never map or answer for generic bevacizumab as BEVACIZUMAB. Preserve the repaired albumin-bound paclitaxel case; globally reinstating `with` as a separator would reopen m3-B1.
+
+### M4-B2 — P1: six-token cap admits qualified longer aliases and distant qualifiers
+
+`POST /api/explore {"text":"generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid"}` now draws **PACLITAXEL POLIGLUMEX / CHEMBL2108628**, with `also:[]`. The same full input is `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` at `/api/resolve`; previous `574097f` refuses with no centre. This exact string was already a m3 supplementary control. [Full current HTTP](attack_http.json), [exact previous/current comparison](names_attack.json).
+
+**Cause:** the stored alias occupies five tokens. `_drug_spans` deliberately retains that full non-overlapping name (`src/query.py:222-239`), but `_qualified_drug_spans` only examines spans up to **six tokens** (`258`: `range(b - a, 7)`). The qualifier plus `of` plus the full alias requires eight. No tested span can contain both `generic version` and the complete drug, so nothing is refused. The map then accepts the bare full alias inside the refused product description. This is a new regression even though the bare poliglumex alias correctly remains supported.
+
+The cap also leaves a simpler inherited bypass: `generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab` (seven tokens) draws BEVACIZUMAB with `also:[]`; the six-token control `generic version of the antibody bevacizumab` refuses. QA `Which trials evaluate generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab?` returns 12 BEVACIZUMAB trial rows. `What targets does generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid act on?` also returns current evidence for CHEMBL2108628 (12 rows, first ACTS_ON edge), despite the qualified product being unresolved. [HTTP responses](attack_http.json).
+
+The **plain qualified phrase** is a new map/QA regression: previous refusal/0 evidence becomes a centre/7 profile-mechanism rows. The longer `What targets ... act on?` question already bypassed the previous linker (12→12 rows), as did `Which trials evaluate generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid?` (4→4). Those prefixed-question failures are inherited. [Exact before/after checks](names_final_checks.json), [plain-phrase comparison](names_attack.json).
+
+**Required outcome:** an arbitrary token-distance ceiling must not turn a known qualified product into a positive originator match. Preserve valid bare multi-token aliases and fail safely when qualification cannot be bound reliably.
+
+### M4-B3 — P1, inherited: the single-token prepass blocks its own extension to the drug
+
+| Exact `/api/explore` text | Current result | Current `/api/ask` reproduction |
+|---|---|---|
+| `biosimilar version of bevacizumab` | BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583; only `biosimilar version` refused | `Which trials evaluate biosimilar version of bevacizumab?` → 12 BEVACIZUMAB trial rows |
+| `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin` | DOXORUBICIN / CHEMBL53463; only `liposomal formulation` refused | `Which trials evaluate liposomal formulation of doxorubicin?` → 3 DOXORUBICIN trial rows |
+
+The existing resolver refuses both complete products. Both parent leaks also occur with `574097f`, so these are **inherited**, not attributed to the latest repair. They nevertheless defeat the approval bar and the new claim that refusal is bound to its drug. [Current HTTP](attack_http.json), [before/after](names_attack.json).
+
+**Cause:** `src/query.py:143-151` marks the qualifier and immediate neighbour before binding. `_drug_spans(tokens, refused)` skips those tokens (`252`, `225-229`), and every candidate crossing them is rejected by `span & refused` (`264`). Thus the helper cannot extend a previously recognized qualifier across `version of` or `formulation of` to its drug. These examples fit within six tokens; raising only the cap does not fix them.
+
+The two-product input `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin and biosimilar version of bevacizumab` links **both parent drugs**, selects DOXORUBICIN and puts BEVACIZUMAB in `also`. By contrast, adjacent `liposomal doxorubicin and biosimilar bevacizumab` correctly refuses both. [Two-product controls](names_attack.json). The m3 hyphenated control `paclitaxel with albumin-bound nanoparticles` also still leaks PACLITAXEL: its one-token qualifier cannot extend across the pre-refused neighbour `with`. The unhyphenated fixed example is therefore not a general solution. [Exact prior control](names_new_boundaries.json).
+
+**Required outcome:** single-token and multi-token qualification need one consistent binding process; already refused qualifier tokens must not make their own qualified drug available as a positive hit.
+
+## No-relink and question-answering assessment
+
+The refusal blockers above reproduce even when matched spans may be relinked in an otherwise identical HEAD linker. They are not caused by the no-relink repair.
+
+- All **7 actual demo questions**, sent over HTTP, have complete response objects identical to the original `bfcb83d` query module. They are also identical to immediate predecessor `574097f` in the focused QA comparison. [HTTP demo evidence](demo_results.json), [focused comparison](names_attack.json).
+- Across **2,584 unique multi-token alias keys**, 402 entity lists change; none changes the first accepted identity or introduces a new Drug. The common change removes an embedded parent Drug/company name. Bare salt/full-name identity is preserved. [Alias comparison](names_attack.json).
+- The isolated counterfactual uses HEAD's exact helpers, dictionaries and data, and changes only `(refused | matched)` back to `refused` in the overlap guard (`src/query.py:168`). **1,648 questions**: 28 completely identical, 1,596 entity-metadata-only changes, 24 semantic changes, 0 exceptions. It covers the **400 alias keys changed specifically by this toggle × four QA templates, plus 48 company/target/mixed controls**. This differs from the 402 aliases changed by the full previous-to-current repair comparison above. [Reproducer](qa_relink_sweep.py), [all inputs and changed answers](qa_relink_sweep.json).
+- The 24 semantic changes remove a Company read from inside a stored full Drug alias, switching `company_portfolio` to `drug_profile`. For example `Tell me about GSK 1120212` previously used nested GSK and returned 12 GlaxoSmithKline portfolio rows; current returns TRAMETINIB's three profile/mechanism rows. `Tell me about EPOETIN BETA` stops treating BETA as company `ORG:beta`. These changes agree with the stored full aliases; they are not adverse regressions found by this review.
+- Explicit `What trials evaluate erlotinib hydrochloride and erlotinib?` still links both separately named records. Multi-entity target/company questions and mixed accepted/refused inputs were checked separately; passing demo/salt checks do not excuse the subject-switch failures above.
+
+**Conclusion for this isolated change:** no adverse no-relink QA regression was found in the exercised corpus. This is a bounded result, not proof for every possible sentence.
+
+## Should-fix items and remaining successful checks
+
+1. **Inherited over-refusal of a neighbouring target/company.** `EGFR biosimilar bevacizumab` and `AstraZeneca biosimilar bevacizumab` refuse the entire input; the reversed forms retain EGFR/AstraZeneca. The generic-version equivalents now retain the target/company. This follows the unconditional immediate-neighbour rule at `src/query.py:149-151`; it does not identify the neighbour's type or syntactic role. A `with` between the target/company and qualifier also preserves them. No invented edge is needed for this usability failure. [Order comparisons](names_attack.json), [HTTP with controls](attack_http.json).
+2. **Unchanged qualifications:** unsupported lowercase IDs/Investigator names, raw malformed-request errors, empty-form retention and numeric Infinity are marked NOT FIXED above. None is treated as a new repair regression or an invalid numeric value actually drawn.
+3. **Stored graph guarantees hold in the checked snapshot.** Seven retired nodes, 142 retired edges, zero live edges with retired endpoints, no self-loops. All six node types are covered. PARENT_OF, STUDIES, ASSOCIATED_WITH, EVALUATES, ACTS_ON and role-aware SPONSORS match their endpoints. Trial details, sponsor count/class, Drug global count/max phase and actions match stored values. [Exhaustive evidence](db_validate.json), [snapshot facts](db_snapshot_facts.json).
+4. **Chunking and size:** the 1,001-Drug fixture crosses three count batches with count/weight 1 for every item. Disease MONDO_0005233 returns 2,774 Trials + 300 Targets, 30 each, **18,575 bytes in 72.117 ms** in the recorded call. **24 concurrent requests / 12 workers**, all expected 200 shapes, **598.259 ms** total. These are local samples, not load benchmarks. [Fixtures](db_fixtures.json), [HTTP](http_results.json).
+5. **No writes or model calls on exercised application paths.** Explore issues reads; `Engine.ask` explicitly uses `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:180-181`). The app does not globally open SQLite read-only, so this is a path/integrity observation rather than a database-permission guarantee.
+

+"""Read-only final audit integrity; output confined beside this script."""
+import hashlib
+import json
+from pathlib import Path
+import re
+import socket
+import subprocess
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
+
+def git(*args):
+    proc = subprocess.run(['git', *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
+    return {'exit_code': proc.returncode,
+            'stdout': proc.stdout.decode('utf-8', 'replace'),
+            'stderr': proc.stderr.decode('utf-8', 'replace')}
+
+initial = json.loads((OUT / 'verification_initial.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
+hashes = {name: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / name).read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+          for name in initial['hashes']}
+ports = {}
+for port in (8880, 8881):
+    with socket.socket() as sock:
+        ports[str(port)] = sock.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1', port)) != 0
+report = (OUT / 'REPORT.md').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+links = re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', report)
+missing = [link for link in links
+           if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://'))
+           and link != 'integrity.json' and not (OUT / link).exists()]
+result = {
+    'head': git('rev-parse', 'HEAD'),
+    'head_unchanged': git('rev-parse', 'HEAD')['stdout'] == initial['head']['stdout'],
+    'tracked_diff': git('diff', '--exit-code'),
+    'staged_diff': git('diff', '--cached', '--exit-code'),
+    'status': git('status', '--short'),
+    'hashes_after': hashes,
+    'source_and_database_hashes_unchanged': hashes == initial['hashes'],
+    'owned_ports_closed': ports,
+    'report_links_checked': len(links),
+    'missing_report_links': missing,
+    'report_ends_with_required_verdict': report.rstrip().endswith('VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED'),
+}
+(OUT / 'integrity.json').write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding='utf-8')
+print(json.dumps(result, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))
+assert result['head_unchanged'] and result['source_and_database_hashes_unchanged']
+assert result['tracked_diff']['exit_code'] == result['staged_diff']['exit_code'] == 0
+assert all(ports.values()) and not missing and result['report_ends_with_required_verdict']

+# Independent relation-map review — round 4
+
+Reviewed HEAD: `bf436c6e93c3eef9e115159ede951a5ecaee5472`, 2026-11-03. Immediate before/after comparison: `574097f` → `bf436c6`; accumulated repair review: `git diff bfcb83d...HEAD`. Specification: the user's round-4 brief, original approval bar, [m1](../m1/REPORT.md), [m2b](../m2b/REPORT.md), [m3](../m3/REPORT.md), and `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`.
+
+**Approval remains blocked.** The exact named m3 blockers are fixed, but the new shortest-span rule can bind a qualifier to the wrong drug and map the refused product's originator. The six-token limit also admits a qualified multi-token drug alias that the previous version refused. Separately, the original single-token qualifier pass still prevents a refusal from reaching its drug. These are current-database map and QA reproductions, not malformed-data fixtures or external pharmacology assumptions.

+
+## Specification blockers
+
+### M4-B1 — P1, new regression: equal-length binding refuses the wrong drug
+
+Exact unchanged-server request:
+
+```text
+POST /api/explore
+{"text":"osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab"}
+```
+
+HTTP 200 contains:
+
+```json
+{"center":{"id":"CHEMBL1201583","label":"BEVACIZUMAB","trials":122},
+ "also":[{"refused":"osimertinib with generic version"}]}
+```
+
+This is an excerpt of the full response in [attack_http.json](attack_http.json). With the exact previous linker and previous `_JOINERS`, the centre is **OSIMERTINIB / CHEMBL3353410** and the refused span is **generic version of bevacizumab**. The current full-string resolver returns `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no drug ID. Reversing the drugs (`bevacizumab with generic version of osimertinib`) also selects the qualified second drug. Adding just `a` — `osimertinib with a generic version of bevacizumab` — changes the current result back to OSIMERTINIB. This is a distance/tie artifact, not a product-identity distinction.
+
+| Exact `/api/ask` text | Previous linker's first evidence | Current HTTP first evidence |
+|---|---|---|
+| `Which trials evaluate osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` | `NCT03909334\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL3353410\|NCT03909334` | `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
+| `Which companies sponsor osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` | `ORG:astrazeneca\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL3353410\|` | `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |
+
+Both current answers have 12 evidence rows; status/evidence-count checks alone miss the subject switch. [Current HTTP](attack_http.json), [exact before/after QA](names_final_checks.json).
+
+**Cause:** `with` was removed from `_JOINERS` (`src/query.py:91-95`). `_drug_spans` supplies the first drug before the second (`222-239`). Both `osimertinib with generic version` and `generic version of bevacizumab` are four-token candidate spans. The shortest-span selector iterates drug spans in that order and updates only on strictly shorter length (`255-272`), so the first candidate wins. Its qualifier tokens enter `out`; the correct second candidate is then disallowed by `span & out` (`264`). BEVACIZUMAB remains acceptable at `181-189`, and the map takes it at `src/app.py:88-95`.
+
+Additional m3 controls `osimertinib; generic version of bevacizumab` and `osimertinib， generic version of bevacizumab` change from a whole-input refusal to a BEVACIZUMAB centre. Their separators are not clause boundaries, so the shortest-span algorithm consumes the left drug and leaves the qualified right drug available. The previous over-refusal was a usability failure; the new originator leak is a blocking regression. [Exact comparisons](names_separator_checks.json).
+
+**Required outcome:** bind the qualifier to the product it describes without consuming a separately named valid drug. This input must never map or answer for generic bevacizumab as BEVACIZUMAB. Preserve the repaired albumin-bound paclitaxel case; globally reinstating `with` as a separator would reopen m3-B1.
+
+### M4-B2 — P1: six-token cap admits qualified longer aliases and distant qualifiers
+
+`POST /api/explore {"text":"generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid"}` now draws **PACLITAXEL POLIGLUMEX / CHEMBL2108628**, with `also:[]`. The same full input is `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` at `/api/resolve`; previous `574097f` refuses with no centre. This exact string was already a m3 supplementary control. [Full current HTTP](attack_http.json), [exact previous/current comparison](names_attack.json).
+
+**Cause:** the stored alias occupies five tokens. `_drug_spans` deliberately retains that full non-overlapping name (`src/query.py:222-239`), but `_qualified_drug_spans` only examines spans up to **six tokens** (`258`: `range(b - a, 7)`). The qualifier plus `of` plus the full alias requires eight. No tested span can contain both `generic version` and the complete drug, so nothing is refused. The map then accepts the bare full alias inside the refused product description. This is a new regression even though the bare poliglumex alias correctly remains supported.
+
+The cap also leaves a simpler inherited bypass: `generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab` (seven tokens) draws BEVACIZUMAB with `also:[]`; the six-token control `generic version of the antibody bevacizumab` refuses. QA `Which trials evaluate generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab?` returns 12 BEVACIZUMAB trial rows. `What targets does generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid act on?` also returns current evidence for CHEMBL2108628 (12 rows, first ACTS_ON edge), despite the qualified product being unresolved. [HTTP responses](attack_http.json).
+
+The **plain qualified phrase** is a new map/QA regression: previous refusal/0 evidence becomes a centre/7 profile-mechanism rows. The longer `What targets ... act on?` question already bypassed the previous linker (12→12 rows), as did `Which trials evaluate generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid?` (4→4). Those prefixed-question failures are inherited. [Exact before/after checks](names_final_checks.json), [plain-phrase comparison](names_attack.json).
+
+**Required outcome:** an arbitrary token-distance ceiling must not turn a known qualified product into a positive originator match. Preserve valid bare multi-token aliases and fail safely when qualification cannot be bound reliably.
+
+### M4-B3 — P1, inherited: the single-token prepass blocks its own extension to the drug
+
+| Exact `/api/explore` text | Current result | Current `/api/ask` reproduction |
+|---|---|---|
+| `biosimilar version of bevacizumab` | BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583; only `biosimilar version` refused | `Which trials evaluate biosimilar version of bevacizumab?` → 12 BEVACIZUMAB trial rows |
+| `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin` | DOXORUBICIN / CHEMBL53463; only `liposomal formulation` refused | `Which trials evaluate liposomal formulation of doxorubicin?` → 3 DOXORUBICIN trial rows |
+
+The existing resolver refuses both complete products. Both parent leaks also occur with `574097f`, so these are **inherited**, not attributed to the latest repair. They nevertheless defeat the approval bar and the new claim that refusal is bound to its drug. [Current HTTP](attack_http.json), [before/after](names_attack.json).
+
+**Cause:** `src/query.py:143-151` marks the qualifier and immediate neighbour before binding. `_drug_spans(tokens, refused)` skips those tokens (`252`, `225-229`), and every candidate crossing them is rejected by `span & refused` (`264`). Thus the helper cannot extend a previously recognized qualifier across `version of` or `formulation of` to its drug. These examples fit within six tokens; raising only the cap does not fix them.
+
+The two-product input `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin and biosimilar version of bevacizumab` links **both parent drugs**, selects DOXORUBICIN and puts BEVACIZUMAB in `also`. By contrast, adjacent `liposomal doxorubicin and biosimilar bevacizumab` correctly refuses both. [Two-product controls](names_attack.json). The m3 hyphenated control `paclitaxel with albumin-bound nanoparticles` also still leaks PACLITAXEL: its one-token qualifier cannot extend across the pre-refused neighbour `with`. The unhyphenated fixed example is therefore not a general solution. [Exact prior control](names_new_boundaries.json).
+
+**Required outcome:** single-token and multi-token qualification need one consistent binding process; already refused qualifier tokens must not make their own qualified drug available as a positive hit.
+
+## No-relink and question-answering assessment
+
+The refusal blockers above reproduce even when matched spans may be relinked in an otherwise identical HEAD linker. They are not caused by the no-relink repair.
+
+- All **7 actual demo questions**, sent over HTTP, have complete response objects identical to the original `bfcb83d` query module. They are also identical to immediate predecessor `574097f` in the focused QA comparison. [HTTP demo evidence](demo_results.json), [focused comparison](names_attack.json).
+- Across **2,584 unique multi-token alias keys**, 402 entity lists change; none changes the first accepted identity or introduces a new Drug. The common change removes an embedded parent Drug/company name. Bare salt/full-name identity is preserved. [Alias comparison](names_attack.json).
+- The isolated counterfactual uses HEAD's exact helpers, dictionaries and data, and changes only `(refused | matched)` back to `refused` in the overlap guard (`src/query.py:168`). **1,648 questions**: 28 completely identical, 1,596 entity-metadata-only changes, 24 semantic changes, 0 exceptions. It covers the **400 alias keys changed specifically by this toggle × four QA templates, plus 48 company/target/mixed controls**. This differs from the 402 aliases changed by the full previous-to-current repair comparison above. [Reproducer](qa_relink_sweep.py), [all inputs and changed answers](qa_relink_sweep.json).
+- The 24 semantic changes remove a Company read from inside a stored full Drug alias, switching `company_portfolio` to `drug_profile`. For example `Tell me about GSK 1120212` previously used nested GSK and returned 12 GlaxoSmithKline portfolio rows; current returns TRAMETINIB's three profile/mechanism rows. `Tell me about EPOETIN BETA` stops treating BETA as company `ORG:beta`. These changes agree with the stored full aliases; they are not adverse regressions found by this review.
+- Explicit `What trials evaluate erlotinib hydrochloride and erlotinib?` still links both separately named records. Multi-entity target/company questions and mixed accepted/refused inputs were checked separately; passing demo/salt checks do not excuse the subject-switch failures above.
+
+**Conclusion for this isolated change:** no adverse no-relink QA regression was found in the exercised corpus. This is a bounded result, not proof for every possible sentence.
+
+## Should-fix items and remaining successful checks
+
+1. **Inherited over-refusal of a neighbouring target/company.** `EGFR biosimilar bevacizumab` and `AstraZeneca biosimilar bevacizumab` refuse the entire input; the reversed forms retain EGFR/AstraZeneca. The generic-version equivalents now retain the target/company. This follows the unconditional immediate-neighbour rule at `src/query.py:149-151`; it does not identify the neighbour's type or syntactic role. A `with` between the target/company and qualifier also preserves them. No invented edge is needed for this usability failure. [Order comparisons](names_attack.json), [HTTP with controls](attack_http.json).
+2. **Unchanged qualifications:** unsupported lowercase IDs/Investigator names, raw malformed-request errors, empty-form retention and numeric Infinity are marked NOT FIXED above. None is treated as a new repair regression or an invalid numeric value actually drawn.
+3. **Stored graph guarantees hold in the checked snapshot.** Seven retired nodes, 142 retired edges, zero live edges with retired endpoints, no self-loops. All six node types are covered. PARENT_OF, STUDIES, ASSOCIATED_WITH, EVALUATES, ACTS_ON and role-aware SPONSORS match their endpoints. Trial details, sponsor count/class, Drug global count/max phase and actions match stored values. [Exhaustive evidence](db_validate.json), [snapshot facts](db_snapshot_facts.json).
+4. **Chunking and size:** the 1,001-Drug fixture crosses three count batches with count/weight 1 for every item. Disease MONDO_0005233 returns 2,774 Trials + 300 Targets, 30 each, **18,575 bytes in 72.117 ms** in the recorded call. **24 concurrent requests / 12 workers**, all expected 200 shapes, **598.259 ms** total. These are local samples, not load benchmarks. [Fixtures](db_fixtures.json), [HTTP](http_results.json).
+5. **No writes or model calls on exercised application paths.** Explore issues reads; `Engine.ask` explicitly uses `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:180-181`). The app does not globally open SQLite read-only, so this is a path/integrity observation rather than a database-permission guarantee.
+

KOA Studio / KhOpenApi code-change comparisons do not apply because no application code was changed. [Separate standards review](standards_findings.md).

+All review-created files are under `audit_evidence_claims/m4/`; pre-existing `brief.md` and the live session `log.txt` were not edited by audit scripts. No tracked/staged diff was introduced. Real DB SHA-256 remains `7341c5a7089731f67356da5a588b269d4b69e8e6c4c054acf19aa4ef2837b529`. Audit-owned servers on 8880 and 8881 are closed. [Final integrity](integrity.json), [main server](http_lifecycle.json), [supplemental server](supplemental_http_lifecycle.json), [attack server](attack_http_lifecycle.json), [browser-test server](browser_lifecycle.json).

+**Specification findings: 3 blocking mechanisms — two new regression mechanisms plus an inherited qualifier-extension bypass — and the separately listed should-fix qualifications.** The exact old named m1/m2b/m3 blockers pass; four m3 supplementary inputs develop originator leaks (the two punctuation cases previously refused too broadly). No remediation was applied.

+"""Read-only final audit integrity; output confined beside this script."""
+import hashlib
+import json
+from pathlib import Path
+import re
+import socket
+import subprocess
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
+
+def git(*args):
+    proc = subprocess.run(['git', *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
+    return {'exit_code': proc.returncode,
+            'stdout': proc.stdout.decode('utf-8', 'replace'),
+            'stderr': proc.stderr.decode('utf-8', 'replace')}
+
+initial = json.loads((OUT / 'verification_initial.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
+hashes = {name: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / name).read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+          for name in initial['hashes']}
+ports = {}
+for port in (8880, 8881):
+    with socket.socket() as sock:
+        ports[str(port)] = sock.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1', port)) != 0
+report = (OUT / 'REPORT.md').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+links = re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', report)
+missing = [link for link in links
+           if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://'))
+           and link != 'integrity.json' and not (OUT / link).exists()]
+result = {
+    'head': git('rev-parse', 'HEAD'),
+    'head_unchanged': git('rev-parse', 'HEAD')['stdout'] == initial['head']['stdout'],
+    'tracked_diff': git('diff', '--exit-code'),
+    'staged_diff': git('diff', '--cached', '--exit-code'),
+    'status': git('status', '--short'),
+    'hashes_after': hashes,
+    'source_and_database_hashes_unchanged': hashes == initial['hashes'],
+    'owned_ports_closed': ports,
+    'report_links_checked': len(links),
+    'missing_report_links': missing,
+    'report_ends_with_required_verdict': report.rstrip().endswith('VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED'),
+}
+(OUT / 'integrity.json').write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding='utf-8')
+print(json.dumps(result, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))
+assert result['head_unchanged'] and result['source_and_database_hashes_unchanged']
+assert result['tracked_diff']['exit_code'] == result['staged_diff']['exit_code'] == 0
+assert all(ports.values()) and not missing and result['report_ends_with_required_verdict']
